EXCLUSIVE: Reba McEntire Reveals the Sentimental Style Behind Her ‘African Cowboy’ Home

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Reba McEntire’s home tells a story—and she has a very personal name for the style that brings it all together.

The country star describes her space as an “African cowboy home,” filled with photographs from her travels, rodeo and ranching keepsakes, and reminders of her Oklahoma upbringing.

Speaking exclusively to Realtor.com®, McEntire reveals the sentimental approach behind her decorating choices, the household splurges she considers worth every penny, and why she never wants visitors to feel afraid to get comfortable.

“I'm a country girl at heart and I love having things around me that tell a story,” she says.

For McEntire, that means surrounding herself with belongings that reflect her experiences and the people who matter most to her.

“So when I bring people into my home, I call it my African cowboy home because I've got my pictures from Africa that I took and then I've got my rodeo ranching things that I've had around me all my life,” she explains.

Those personal touches take priority over decorating purely for appearances.

“I've always believed the home ought to reflect the people living in it, not just what somebody thinks looks pretty,” she says.

McEntire’s reflections come as she continues her partnership with Realtor.com, which launched its “Nearly Home” campaign on May 5, 2025.

The sitcom-inspired ads draw on real homebuyers’ experiences, using humor and practical solutions to make the home-buying journey feel more approachable.

In her own home, however, some of the most important belongings are things she could never replace.

“I'm very sentimental, especially when it comes to family keepsakes, pictures, little things that remind me of mom and daddy, of course, memories of growing up in Oklahoma,” she says.

While furniture can come and go, those connections have lasting value.

“You can always buy new furniture, but you can't replace those memories,” she adds.

Her appreciation for home begins the moment she walks through the front door, when she takes a moment to give thanks before settling in.

“Well, I thank the Lord for getting me home safely, and I put down all my stuff and, just get ready for whatever the rest of the day brings,” she shares.

Although family keepsakes are among her most treasured possessions, McEntire also knows the value of investing in everyday comfort.

When it comes to a home splurge that has proved worth every penny, her answer comes easily. “Oh, without a doubt, a comfortable bed,” she says.

For someone who spends time traveling, returning to her own familiar sleeping setup is a pleasure in itself.

“I spent a lot of time traveling and there's nothing better than coming home and crawling into your own bed with your own pillow,” she explains.

Another purchase that earns her approval is a good ice machine—a fitting choice given her fondness for iced tea, which she names as something she always keeps in the refrigerator.

Her favorite room is equally straightforward: “Kitchen.”

But McEntire’s ideal day at home does not require an elaborate itinerary or much fuss. “I think I'm pretty low maintenance, but that might surprise somebody,” she says.

“I'm the happiest in comfortable clothes, no makeup on, just relaxing out on the back porch.” That outdoor retreat includes a particular feature she enjoys. “I've got a real cool porch swing, and, I don't need a lot of fuss,” she adds.

A few simple comforts are enough to keep her content.

“Give me good company, something good to eat, and a little peace and quiet, and I'm a happy camper,” she says.

That easygoing attitude also informs the decorating choices she avoids. McEntire has little interest in interiors that make people feel as though they need to tiptoe around.

“Anything that makes a house look like you're afraid to sit on the furniture or walk on the carpet,” she says of the decorating trend she would never try.

In fact, her biggest houseguest pet peeve is someone who does not settle in.

“Someone who doesn't make themselves at home, you know, come on in, get comfortable,” she says.

Asked to describe her home in two words, McEntire offers an answer that captures that philosophy: “Warm and welcoming.”

The same sense of comfort and belonging underpins the “Nearly Home” campaign, which pairs McEntire’s humor with relatable homebuying situations and tools designed to help people navigate their search.

The campaign launched ahead of McEntire’s role hosting the 2025 Academy of Country Music Awards, connecting with country music fans as the spring home shopping season got underway. McEntire also debuted her newest single live onstage during the awards.

The ads highlight features such as commute filters, which help buyers check drive times from a potential home, and dynamic maps that let them explore factors including lot size and schools. Through familiar challenges and accessible solutions, the campaign aims to make finding a home feel easier—and more fun.

Now, McEntire is bringing her storytelling to NAR NXT in New Orleans. She will appear in the general session, “The Real Deal: Strategy & Storytelling With Reba McEntire,” on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026 at the Ernest N. Morial Convention Center.

Attendees can visit Realtor.com’s booth, No. 700, on the expo floor on Friday, Nov. 6, for a chance to win a meet-and-greet with McEntire following her Saturday session.

Those stopping by the booth on Saturday and Sunday can also enter for a chance to win officially signed Reba merchandise.

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Meerkat&#8217;s Dustin Black Makes an Art of Staying Curious

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Creative energy doesn’t ebb when the world stops. So when the pandemic stalled agency work in 2020, Dustin Black turned his lifelong pursuit of creativity on his home.

The career art director—recently named chief creative officer and partner at full-service Minneapolis creative agency Meerkat (formerly Preston Spire)—has twin daughters, who were 6 years old at the time. He’d been planning to turn their family mantra—“Be brave, be curious, be kind”—into a giant banner for their home, and suddenly had the time to make it.

Using felt sourced from New York-based manufacturer Oxford Pennant, Black’s banner infused some much-needed cheer into their basement. He also shared the finished product with the company.

“Oxford Pennant just absolutely loved it,” he said, so much so that they began producing a pennant using Black’s design—and offered him a supply of felt for future projects.

In fact, Black did have another idea in the works: He’d been sculpting a 5-foot-long fish out of foam that was in need of scales. As it happened, at-home cutting machine maker Cricut had also taken an interest in Black’s banner and offered to sponsor his work.

All that was left was for Black to take every hands-on art lesson, from fingerpainting as a toddler to his double major in art at the University of Nebraska-Lincoln, and apply it to a new kind of blank slate. “I had to figure out, what would a felt fish look like? I started playing, and I suddenly had my first fish covered in felt scales,” he said.

Black has been chasing new creative highs ever since, from making ever-more complex scales to cladding his fish in felt flowers instead, then turning to sculpting birds. Each sculpture begins with elaborate sketches, then Black figures out the size and frames it out in metal, wood, and foam.

The Cricut does the tedious work of cutting out the felt, then Black glues on each piece and further decorates it with paint and other materials. Everything is done inside his 25-square-foot home office on nights and weekends.

Since the beginning, he’s involved his girls to “show them it’s OK to try something new and fail” (they now have their own art projects as well). Black also documents the creative process of each piece on Instagram, including his mistakes and how he moved on from them.

“I’m trying to put out there the notion of we’re all beginners, and we’re all figuring it out, and it might look good in the end but it took a lot to get there,” he explained.

His sculptures have been exhibited in galleries, at events and, most recently, for a year at Minneapolis-Saint Paul International Airport. Having them in a public space “has been a lot of fun because I get random texts from people: ‘Look at what my family group chat is talking about!’” he said.

The pursuit of new shapes, styles, and designs is what drives Black, which is why he’s resisted taking commissions that would dictate what he makes—or worse, copy his previous work.

His latest project, an eagle with feathers made of polymer clay that Black hand-painted, has pride of place inside the Meerkat offices. With the widespread suspicion of AI in creative work, Black knows the value in clients being able to see and touch a piece of art made in-house.

He also hopes it inspires the kind of creativity that drives good work: “We need to be doing new things. We need to be solving complex problems. And we need to use curiosity to approach it.”

UK now puts AI’s 2030 boost at £120bn, not the £400bn from a Google report

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Kanishka Narayan told Parliament the higher number came from a Google-commissioned report by Public First. The government’s own model puts AI's gain at £55bn to £140bn in 2030.

Kanishka Narayan, the UK’s minister for artificial intelligence.

The UK government now expects AI to add £55bn to £140bn to the economy by 2030. Its central estimate is £120bn. Its AI Opportunities Action Plan put the gain at £400bn, and AI minister Kanishka Narayan has now confirmed that Google commissioned the report behind that number.

Narayan gave both figures in a written parliamentary answer on 26 October. He became the UK’s first cabinet-level AI minister in July. Dr Ben Spencer, the Conservative shadow AI minister, had asked what evidence sat behind the plan’s £400bn claim.

“The £400 billion figure was drawn from a publicly available 2023 report commissioned by Google and produced by Public First,” Narayan wrote.

He said the government had since made its own assessment. It found that AI adoption and data investment could add 0.4 to 1.3 percentage points to UK productivity growth.

“These estimates are hugely uncertain, but this would be the equivalent of adding £55-140 billion to UK GVA in 2030,” he wrote.

GVA, or gross value added, measures the value of what an economy produces. The answer did not withdraw the £400bn figure.

Google published the higher number in July 2023. It came from an economic impact report that the consultancy Public First compiled. The report said that “under the right conditions”, AI could create over £400bn in value for the UK by 2030.

Tech investor Matt Clifford wrote the action plan, which the government published in January 2025.

The government’s lower range comes from its own model. The Department for Science, Innovation and Technology sets it out in an explainer for its AI Skills Boost. The model runs three scenarios, for slow, medium and rapid adoption, using OECD productivity estimates.

Ben Gartside reported for WIRED that this is the first time on record the government has set the two figures side by side. It is also the first time it has said Google commissioned the report. WIRED found that Skills England still published the £400bn figure months after ministers had the lower one.

Public First, now called PF Global, told WIRED that its 2023 model assumed faster adoption than the government now expects. Its current central estimate for 2030 is much closer to the government’s figure, it said, with more growth after that. The firm also said it made its own judgements in that research.

Conservative peer Lord Holmes of Richmond told WIRED he asked ministers where the figure came from in January 2025. He got no reply, he said.

Dame Chi Onwurah chairs the Commons science and technology committee. She told WIRED that overstating what technology can deliver risks undermining the case for change. Sumedha Deshmukh of the Ada Lovelace Institute told WIRED that independent experts should test such forecasts, and that the government should measure the promised gains.

Spencer has kept asking. On 4 November, Narayan answered a follow-up question from him by pointing back to an earlier answer on AI skills. The same week, Narayan warned that a serious cyber breach in the UK is “extremely likely”.

The faction is due an update in spring 2027

It's become a long-running joke that Warhammer 40K Blood Angels fans keep getting ignored or neglected, but that could be due to change early next year.

In a surprise announcement on Warhammer Community, the team revealed that Codex: Blood Angels would be arriving as of spring 2027 alongside an undisclosed number of new Warhammer 40K models. Yes, this could end up being yet another snub with a character or two like before. But specific wording in the announcement makes me hope it'll be a bigger drop. The title promises "Big news for Blood Angels," after all. It also points out that "Alongside [the Codex] will be new miniatures." Miniatures, plural.

However, That same post provided a look at the first new model; the returning Sanguinary High Priest Corbulo, bearer of the Red Grail and seen strutting his stuff in the image above.

Although there haven't been any bespoke Blood Angels models for a while, the faction has been a big focus of the current 11th Edition. Indeed, they were the poster-army for the Armageddon box set (which you can currently grab for 15% less at Amazon).

I'll admit, I could be applying clown makeup to myself right now or injecting copious amounts of hopium. Especially because last edition offered crumbs and even the upcoming Christmas Blood Angels Battleforce (which has just been announced) was a very weak entry, even compared to other boxes dropping at the same time. But making such a song and dance about this new Blood Angels launch, and their prominence in the current Warhammer 40K edition, make me hope they'll enjoy the same treatment the Dark Angels got at the beginning of 10th Edition – new heroes and troops alike.

It won't just be Blood Angels enjoying reinforcements either; spring will apparently bring us new releases for "Xenos, Chaos, and non-Space Marine Imperial armies." Could that be when we'll receive the long-promised Steel Legion revamp?

For now, we'll just have to wait and see. As it stands, one of the last major launches of the year looks to be Kill Team: Armageddon and its kick-ass Legion of the Damned.

Ripple's Dubai Event Presence Gets High-Profile Boost From Emirates Chairman

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ripple received high-profile attention during the recently concluded Dubai FinTech Summit, which took place November 2-3, 2026, with Emirates Airline Chairman and Chief Executive Sheikh Ahmed bin Saeed Al Maktoum visiting the company's booth during the event.

In a recent X post about the event, Reece Merrick, Ripple's Senior Executive Officer/Managing Director, Middle East and Africa, commented that it was an incredible week at the summit. He added that it was a busy couple of days marked by immense interest and strong engagement around Ripple's offerings across the Middle East region.

What an incredible week at the @DubaiFinTechSum! A busy couple of days marked by immense interest and strong engagement around @Ripple's offerings across the region. We remain deeply committed to scaling our footprint in the Middle East out of our @DIFC hub. Merrick also noted that the company is still committed to growing its presence in the Middle East through its hub at the Dubai International Financial Centre (DIFC). Ripple's exhibition stand was also attended by DIFC Governor Essa Kazim and DIFC CEO Arif Amiri, as well as Emirates Airline Chairman and Chief Executive Sheikh Ahmed bin Saeed Al Maktoum.

The high-profile visit was a highlight of Ripple's presence at the summit, offering a platform to connect with regional stakeholders and demonstrate its financial technology solutions. Although the visit does not, by itself, signal a new partnership or agreement, it highlights the level of institutional and executive engagement surrounding Ripple at the event.

As reported, Merrick highlighted his participation at the Dubai FinTech Summit as part of a panel that explored how digital assets could scale alongside executives from BlackRock, Standard Chartered and Robinhood.

Ripple's commitment to the Middle East as a central region for its growth strategy remains steadfast, with its presence in the DIFC serving as a springboard for fostering relationships and identifying potential opportunities within the market.

Ripple in 2026 said it was expanding in the UAE with the opening of its Middle East and Africa (MEA) regional headquarters in the Dubai International Financial Centre (DIFC). The announcement highlighted the strategic footprint of the firm in the country.

In 2024, Ripple announced a partnership with the DIFC Innovation Hub—the Dubai International Financial Centre's (DIFC) vibrant innovation ecosystem—to promote blockchain innovation in the UAE.

These 6 Utility Stocks Offer Income and a Link to Power Demand

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

NextEra Energy is leaning into electricity demand, but it isn't the only way to play demand growth.

NextEra Energy (NEE -0.23%) and Dominion Energy (D -0.16%) plan to merge, as NextEra seeks to tap into the massive growth in electricity demand it expects. Duke Energy (DUK +0.38%), The Southern Company (SO +0.24%), American Electric Power (AEP -1.91%), and Xcel Energy (XEL -0.16%) are all hoping to do the same thing. And there's a huge opportunity, with electricity demand expected to increase 60% between 2025 and 2045. To put that demand into perspective, it increased by only 10% between 2005 and 2025.

Here's a look at each of these six large dividend-paying U.S. utilities and why you might want to consider buying each one.

NextEra Energy offers a 2.8% dividend yield. That's in line with the industry average of roughly 2.8%. However, NextEra is anything but average; it is the world's largest utility by market cap. And it plans to get even larger through the proposed acquisition of Dominion (a utility discussed in more detail below).

But the real story with NextEra is that the business has two parts. The foundation is its regulated utility assets, today largely composed of its Florida Power & Light operations. In addition, NextEra operates one of the largest wind and solar power businesses on the planet. This business uses long-term, market-based contracts. That has been an important growth engine for the company. If you are looking for a single utility investment, NextEra covers a lot of ground.

Duke Energy offers a 3.3% yield. In recent years, it has become a leaner business, selling its contracted power assets to focus on its regulated operations. It operates across the electric and natural gas segments of the industry, with regulated utilities in North Carolina, South Carolina, Florida, Indiana, Ohio, and Kentucky. It is also one of the world's top 10 largest utilities by market cap.

Duke believes its $100 billion capital spending plan over the next five years will support annual earnings growth of 5% to 7%. The company generally has constructive relationships with its regulators, which increases the likelihood of success on this front. And in many regions where it operates, there are mechanisms that automatically adjust rates based on spending. It is a solid option for investors seeking a regulated utility.

The Southern Company has a yield of 3.2%. It has either increased or maintained its dividend for 79 years, making it a highly reliable income stock. The most notable event in the company's recent history was the completion of Vogtle 3 and 4, the newest nuclear power plants built in the United States. That sets the company up well to supply the electricity demand that it sees ahead.

That said, the company has an $81 billion capital investment plan that it believes will support long-term earnings growth of 7% to 8%. Like Duke, the company has electric and gas operations. Its regulated electric business spans Alabama, Georgia, and Mississippi, with a smaller, unregulated business that sells power under long-term contracts. Its modest natural gas distribution operations expand the company's reach into Illinois, Tennessee, and Virginia. Like NextEra and Duke, it ranks among the world's 10 largest utilities.

American Electric Power offers a 3% yield. It is projecting annual earnings growth of 7%-9% through 2030, powered by a $78 billion capital investment plan. And, like the three companies above, it ranks among the world's 10 largest utilities.

The company has an extremely broad footprint, with regulated electric operations across 11 states: Arkansas, Indiana, Kentucky, Louisiana, Michigan, Ohio, Oklahoma, Tennessee, Texas, Virginia, and West Virginia. It also has a large transmission business. It is probably one of the most diversified electric utility options here.

Like Duke Energy, Dominion Energy has been slimming down to its core utility operations. The most recent move was to sell the majority of its regulated natural gas utilities to Enbridge (ENB -1.37%). But there's an even larger change on the horizon, as Dominion has agreed to be acquired by NextEra Energy. Dominion's yield is 3.8%.

The big draw for NextEra Energy is twofold: Dominion has a large nuclear power business, and it operates in one of the world's most important data center markets. Notably, buying Dominion will catapult NextEra into the number two spot in nuclear power in the U.S. market. It will also expand NextEra's business to include Virginia, North Carolina, and South Carolina. That said, Dominion is really a special-situations play at this point given the pending acquisition, so it probably won't appeal to most investors despite its relatively high yield.

Xcel Energy yields 2.9%. It recently updated its five-year capital investment plan, extending it through 2031. The update includes $70 billion in capital spending that management believes will drive earnings growth of 9% or more. That's particularly high for a utility.

Notably, Xcel operates in regions well-suited to developing solar and wind power. That gives it an edge as the world shifts toward renewable power generation. With operations across eight Western and Midwestern states, Xcel is probably best suited for investors who favor growth.

Electricity is an increasingly important source of power. In fact, the modern world can't work without reliable electricity. All six utilities on this list provide reliable power, though each does so slightly differently and in different regions. If you are looking to generate income from the increasing demand for power, any one of them could be a good starting place for investor preferences that range from growth to income to special situations.

Reuben Gregg Brewer has positions in Enbridge and Southern Company. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Enbridge and NextEra Energy. The Motley Fool recommends Dominion Energy and Duke Energy. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

DigiCert admits 16-month security lapse exposed customer information

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Botched server move left the door open, and DigiCert took months to notice someone had wandered in

DigiCert has admitted that a botched server migration left an internal system exposed to the internet for more than a year, allowing an unauthorized user to access customer data, including passports, driver's licenses, and other identity documents.

The certificate authority has begun notifying affected customers but has yet to disclose how many people had their information exposed.

In an email seen by The Register, DigiCert confirmed the compromised information included names, business email addresses, phone numbers, and business addresses. Some records may also have contained passports, driver's licenses, utility bills, or other identification documents.

"We are completing a review of identification documents to determine the specific individuals involved," DigiCert told those affected, adding that it would notify those people once the review was complete.

DigiCert's incident report traces the blunder back to February 2025, when an internal business system was moved from a VPN-protected network to a different availability zone. That change also opened the system up to the public internet, where it sat for more than 16 months without DigiCert realizing.

The archive used the same account management platform as DigiCert's customer-facing products, including a self-registration feature that was never supposed to be available on the internal system. This allowed outsiders to create accounts without multifactor authentication and, thanks to a separate permissions flaw, use them to browse archived customer records.

DigiCert found the unauthorized accounts on June 18, 2026, but initially dismissed them as harmless because they weren't assigned to an application. A security researcher raised the alarm about the account creation issue three days later, but it took until July 2 for DigiCert to shut it down.

Unfortunately, someone had already been poking around.

It wasn't until October 12 that DigiCert began investigating whether customer information had actually been accessed. On October 19, it confirmed that an unauthorized account had accessed a subset of archived records between June 27 and July 2.

DigiCert described the affected records as a "small proportion" of its archive but hasn't disclosed how many customers or individuals were caught up in the breach. The company did not respond to The Register’s questions at the time of publication.

DigiCert's postmortem makes for uncomfortable reading. The server move didn't get a proper security review, monitoring failed to pick up suspicious activity, and the access controls weren't much use against self-registered accounts. The company has since switched off self-registration, tightened network access, and added monitoring that might have come in handy a few months earlier.

Certificate authority operations, CertCentral, and DigiCert ONE were unaffected, the company said, adding that customer credentials and issued certificates remain secure. It also found no evidence that the unauthorized account had been used to issue, revoke, or validate certificates.

That’s good news for anyone worried about their certificates, but less so for those who handed DigiCert a copy of their passport. ®

GTA San Andreas CJ Actor Shuts Down Rumor About Money Dispute

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto: San Andreas star Young Maylay has shot down the long-standing rumours that he was unhappy with his pay for playing CJ.

As the insurmountable hype for GTA 6 has more than reminded us, Rockstar’s mainstay series is easily the biggest in all of gaming. That makes it a massive opportunity for anyone who appears in them, especially when it comes to playing the leading characters, like Tommy, CJ, Michael, Niko, and Franklin.

We’ve seen both the good and the bad of what it means to star in a Rockstar game, from Roger Clark’s love of everything Arthur Morgan to Steven Ogg’s annoyance at mostly being known for Trevor. On the more serious side of things, some actors have even been vocally dissatisfied with the end result of their work, which thankfully isn’t the case for one of GTA’s most iconic protagonists.

For some time now, it’s appeared that Young Maylay, who played Carl Johnson in San Andreas, was unhappy with his time on what many call the best GTA game. The beef all started in 2020 when Maylay called Rockstar “culture vultures” for profiting off their depiction of gang culture, which many took as a sign he felt undercompensated.

Sony is right - Grand Theft Auto 6 is probably going to play best on PlayStation.

While it might be true that Maylay isn’t keen on working with Rockstar again, it isn't about the money. Earlier this week, Michael de Santa actor Ned Luke released the latest episode of The Final Mission, which featured Young Maylay. As pointed out by Twitter user papaRPG_, Maylay took the chance to dispel the rumours of a pay dispute.

Maylay called the rumours a “misconception” and that he knew what he was getting paid going into it because of the contract he signed. CJ’s actor also noted that no one knows what he got for his time on the game, and that Rockstar gave him “bonuses out of nowhere” and a care package when the game released, which he described as giving him “more” than he expected.

It was also revealed that Rockstar had already made CJ’s character model when Mayday got to the project, so his likeness wasn’t used like for other GTA protagonists.

After pointing out that Rockstar could have made anyone famous with the role of CJ, Maylay concluded by calling the pay rumours “bulls**t” and that he wouldn’t have done the project if he wasn’t happy with it. It still seems from the actor’s earlier comments that he’s not entirely happy with Rockstar as a whole, but the payday isn't why.

Of course, it’s not like there isn’t a precedent for GTA actors being unhappy with the pay for their work. Niko Bellic actor Michael Hollick was openly against the $100,000 flat rate he received for 15 months of work on, at the time, the biggest game in the world, although a lot of that blame was laid on SAG instead of Rockstar specifically.

Two hours with Xenoverse 3’s Parallel Quests made me sure that the full game is absolutely going to ruin my life.

Tesla to wind down Cybertruck production in Texas next year to ramp Cybercab, Musk says

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Tesla CEO Elon Musk on Friday said the electric vehicle maker will "be winding down" production of the Cybertruck at its Texas gigafactory around the middle of next year.

Musk, who hailed the Cybertruck as the future of the pickup truck segment, said in a post on X early Friday morning that the end of production at the massive facility would make room for production of its Cybercab robotaxi.

The Cybertruck is exclusively produced at the company's plant in Texas alongside the Tesla Model Y and two-seat Cybercab, which Musk has touted as a crucial product for the automaker moving forward.

Musk did not say whether the company would produce the Cybertruck at a different facility, but he later responded to a user on X who called the update an "end of an era" and said the Cybertruck was the "best vehicle Tesla has ever made."

"True, but the Cybertruck fleet will be around for a long time," Musk said.

Tesla began Cybertruck deliveries in late 2023. The company did not immediately respond to a request for comment from CNBC.

The Cybertruck and its polarizing design gained a niche following, but it did not achieve mass-market sales, despite Musk's pre-launch prediction that annual sales would eventually total 250,000.

"Trucks have been the same for a very long time," Musk said when the truck was unveiled in 2019. "Like 100 years. Trucks have been basically the same. We want to try something different."

While Tesla does not break out its sales by model or region, Cox Automotive estimated sales of the Cybertruck last year at 20,237 units in the U.S., down 48% from 38,965 in 2024.

One big buyer of the Cybertruck last year was Elon Musk's aerospace and tech conglomerate, SpaceX, which spent $131 million buying up the angular steel trucks at Tesla's suggested retail price, according to a SpaceX financial filing.

The distinct look of the vehicle made some owners a target of rude gestures and more serious road rage, one owner told CNBC last May, after Musk became the biggest funder of Donald Trump's presidential campaign and led the Department of Government Efficiency, an effort to slash the federal workforce and regulations.

Wired wrote in an October 2025 feature story, "There is likely no object that feels more emblematic of US president Donald Trump's return to the White House than the Tesla Cybertruck." The magazine interviewed owners who described being spit on and sworn at due to their choice of vehicle.

The Cybertruck's specifications also fell short of those Tesla advertised early on, and in some cases fell short of those offered by leading fuel-burning pickups. There have also been many quality and production problems associated with the vehicles.

The 2024 Tesla Cybertruck was the subject of 11 voluntary recalls, according to the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration website. Issues ranged from a "trapped" acceleration pedal risk that could cause unintended acceleration, to software defects and an inverter fault that could result in sudden loss of drive power.

I Know Scott Caan Really Wanted To Play A Different Character On NCIS: New York After Hawaii 5-0, But I Have To Be Honest

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

Blue Bloods’ Jamie Actor Made A Promise About His Return In The Spinoff, And I’m So Relieved

NCIS' Latest Celebrity Cameo Was A Classic Callback, But It Confused Me For Most Of The Episode

I Like Netflix's Minimum Wage, But I Wish One Character Got A Bigger Spotlight

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Different, sure, but also kind of the same (in a good way).

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I’m new to the NCIS franchise. Even though the O.G. has been on since George W. Bush’s first term, I’ve only recently gotten into NCIS: New York. One of the reasons I decided to give it a go on the 2026 TV schedule was for Scott Caan. I was a big fan of Hawaii Five-0, especially the early seasons, and his character is great on it. In fact, my first dip into the NCIS universe was a Five-0 crossover with NCIS: Los Angeles. Despite appearing in that crossover, Caan isn’t reprising the role in NCIS: New York (as LL Cool J is). However, the character is pretty darn similar.

Caan played his Five-0 character, Danny “Danno” Williams, in that crossover episode, of course, but when he signed on to NCIS: New York, he did so in order to play a new character named Nick Schaeffer. I fully appreciate that Caan wants to avoid getting stuck in a rut playing Danny across franchises, but let’s be honest here: they are basically the same guy.

Both characters are cops from the Tri-State area. Danny’s backstory on Hawaii Five-0 was that he was a divorced police detective who had to move to Hawaii to be close to his ex-wife and daughter. Nick is an NCIS Special Agent from New York City. Now, people in both NY and NJ will tell you they are different culturally, but those nuances are lost on most of America. Both Danny and Nick have a bit of snark in everything they do; they are both tough as nails, and they are both completely loyal to their partners. LL Cool J’s Sam Hanna in NCIS: New York and Steve McGarrett (Alex O'Loughlin) in Five-0.

So, again, basically the same character, but there are some fun differences. Danny was kind of a prick in the early seasons of Five-0. He wasn’t happy about living in Hawaii, he didn’t acclimate to the state's unique culture, and he was pretty much just grumpy about it all the time. He was snarky and prickly. Nick on NCIS: NY, on the other hand, is still a quippy partner who isn’t afraid to be honest with his team, but he’s a genuinely nice guy. He’s snarky, but he’s far from prickly. I’m into it.

Scott Caan is great at playing this kind of role, so I’m really happy to see him doing it again. This article isn’t meant to be any kind of indictment, indeed, quite the opposite. I may miss Danno on Hawaii Five-0, but having Nick in New York is great.

Procedurals, like NCIS: New York, for me, live or die on the strength of the cast. The stories are good, but they are like the hot dog when I’m really just looking for a vehicle for mustard. The characters are the mustard. The mustard is what makes hot dogs great, and it’s Caan, LL, and the rest of the cast, including Jennifer Beals, Jacqueline Byers, and Shane Harper, who are going to keep me tuning in week after week.

If you'd like to tune in week after week, you can catch new episodes of NCIS: New York on CBS every Tuesday at 9 p.m. ET, and you can stream it the next day with a Paramount+ subscription.

From Hibs casual to a sold-out Hydro - the incredible story of bare-knuckle boxing's Leith Warrior

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The hands might tell a story although Lewis Knox will hope they know when to stop blabbing. After all, he doesn't want his tools of the trade to betray him.

These fists helped him survive on Leith’s mean streets. As a former Hibs casual, he once used them to defend his club’s honour.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

In a more legitimate context, one less likely to interest the authorities, they are crucial for his current job installing sofas up and down the country. And more pressingly, in Glasgow on Saturday, these hands will be used as weapons with the aim of drawing blood.

“All legal and above board,” Knox clarifies. Welcome to the world of “BKFC”, which stands for Bare Knuckle Fighting Championship. The promotion, co-owned by controversial UFC star Conor McGregor, hits Scotland for the first time this weekend.

Only the All Blacks’ visit to Murrayfield, Celtic v Kilmarnock and possibly Aberdeen v Hibs will attract a bigger crowd in a sporting context.

Still, it’s guaranteed none of those involved in these fixtures will have spent the start of this week lugging sofas up and down stairwells. “I install them into houses from Berwick-Upon-Tweed to near enough Stonehaven," says Knox. "12-hour shifts, 7 am to 7 pm. Four days on, four days off. But I am still training every night. As well as being a dad of three."

Even the All Blacks, who have been utilising the gym where Knox has been training under the watchful eye of Ali Thomson, might wince at the thought of this schedule. Thomson describes Knox as a "phenomenon". He complains his own arms are aching from doing pads with the focused fighter in the run-up to this weekend.

"People say things are impossible,” ponders Knox. “But you know if you really want it? Things can be done. My mum, my dad, my family. They all support me. Everyone is tuned into it. They all try to help out. This is not an overnight thing, this has been three years in the making to get to where I am now. I have worked so hard."

He was in Fife as recently as Monday delivering sofas but who knows where he will be this time next year? The bare-knuckle scene is taking off, boosted by McGregor's involvement. "I'd like to turn pro," says Knox, 29. Remember that Hearts fan ridiculed for claiming on camera that he’d be willing to have his hands chopped off if it meant his team winning the league?

Knox loves Hibs. He was thrilled by last weekend’s League Cup semi-final win over Hearts at Hampden, which he watched on TV because he was deep in fight camp. However, he can’t afford to strike any bargain involving the prospect of losing his hands. As a bare-knuckle boxer on the cusp of his biggest ever fight, they are, after his three children, among the most precious things on earth to him.

Perhaps a little Atrixo might be in order. They are battered, bruised and a little disfigured. Hardly surprising really, since they are used to pound his opponents. “Bone on bone,” he says. “There is nothing more raw.” He reports that he hasn't broken his hands in his last three fights. "They are solid, like sledgehammers," he says.

Rather than slathering them with hand cream, Knox is considering the old fighters’ trick of sticking his hands into a bucket of sand – or rice. “It strengthens up the skin,” he says.

He broke two knuckles in his very first (official) bare-knuckle fight three years ago this month. Slightly horrifyingly, these knuckles now appear to have disappeared completely. “Yeah, disintegrated,” he says. “Two knuckles have gone, one has dipped (into the hand). As for my pinkie, I can’t stretch it out."

Knox demonstrates this inability to extend his little finger in the café where we are sitting. The Leith Warrior, as he’s known, has ordered a flat white with a shot of caramel (there was still plenty of time to make the required fighting weight of 66 kgs). If not for the damage to his hands, he seems incredibly unwarrior-like.

Softly spoken and trim, almost delicate-looking, the tattoos extending past his collar line on his neck are perhaps the main evidence of a life lived on the edge.

“I always wanted tattoos,” he says. As one suspects is the case with many people, he first succumbed to the needle and ink on holiday in Magaluf.

“A star on my chest and a Hibs tattoo on the back of my calf,” he says. “I thought I had to get serious ones after that. Ones devoted to my gran and many grandad, who both played huge parts in my life from a young age. I also have a gladiator on my arm and neck tattoos…It has become part of the job!”

As for the colours he sports when competing in the circular four-rope ring, which is designed to encourage fast-paced bouts, it wasn’t up for discussion. “Green and white, I always represent where I am from,” he says. “And a Hibs badge on my shorts. I am never not representing Leith.

“I grew up in Leith, a lot of gang culture, an environment of violence as a young boy,” he continues. “You had to be streetwise from a young age I suppose. Leith has its good and bad sides. For me, it’s one of the best places in the world. But it can be one of the worst places in the world.

"Knife crime, drugs. It’s a mix of cultures, backgrounds. I grew up in all of that. But I was never ultra-violent.”

His mother, Niki, once ran the Junction bar. Knox enjoys an obvious status in the area. A taxi driver beeps his horn as soon as he steps out of the door of our meeting place on Easter Road. Cross Knoxy and you cross Leith.

He knows people, Irvine Welsh included. The author – a more than decent boxer himself - has been mentoring Knox before his featherweight division fight against Tommy “Chaos” Carus from Liverpool at the OVO Hydro on Saturday night. Welsh has already featured in an Instagram video promoting Knox’s fight, playing the role of his manager.

“Oi, Knoxy!” he calls out after taking a phone call. “Get your dancing shoes on! We’ve got ourselves a pagger.”

Knox v Carus, or the Leith Warrior v Chaos, or indeed Scotland v England, is the third fight of 11 on a bill topped by Sean Weir’s light heavyweight title bout with Leigh Cohoon, a late stand-in for Irishman Conor ‘Da Crook’ Cooke.

Cooke’s team cited concerns about the venue layout and ticketing arrangements for their withdrawal. Some have interpreted this as another way of saying they were worried sectarian/football-related hooliganism – Weir was once a member of the Rangers Riot Crew casuals group - could flare.

Almost 11,000 are expected to converge on the Hydro for the event. BKFC is dedicated to “fighter safety”. According to their official bumph, the organisation prides itself in being “the truest form of bare-knuckle fighting”. Fighters are not allowed to wrap their hands to within one inch of the knuckle. This weekend’s spectacle is the first legally sanctioned and regulated bare knuckle event in Scotland.

For the uninitiated, the fights last for five two-minute rounds. If there isn’t a knockout, it tends to be fairly obvious who’s won.

“Cuts,” says Knox. “It comes down to facial damage. Because in boxing or any gloved fight, you can punch them ten times with a glove and not make a mark, you can punch them once with a bare knuckle and split someone’s head open.

“It is about getting as much of that in in a short period of time. The first punch usually sets the tone for the fight. It depends on where you catch him, I suppose. It is all about just getting in there and being horrible.”

It’s easy to question the ethics of unpadded pugilism. However, defenders of this ancient and previously underground practice insist it’s a safer, more honest form of boxing. "It is better for the brain, much better," claims Knox. "In boxing you get the double shock with the glove – boom, boom. It rattles the brain. You don’t see the damage facially necessarily. With bare knuckle, bone is hitting bone. That’s it. The facial damage will be shown. Internally your brain is not getting rattled for 12 rounds.

“It is five rounds of two minutes. If you last that, although you might get a few cuts, your brain is fine. To me, my brain feels fine! In my 50s and 60s I will still have a functional brain. I might have a few scars and a few missing teeth but I move pretty well (to avoid the punches). My nose is still straight...”

Inside the ring might be a veritable haven as the evening hots up on Saturday. “It is like any combat sport,” says Knox. “The crowd can be unpredictable. A bit like a football crowd. I just hope that because this event is so big for Scotland and so big for the bare-knuckle scene and for lads like myself that nothing serious kicks off. There will be some ‘characters’ there, you always get the odd person. You get that anywhere.”

Knox anticipates an unholy commotion when the first bars of Born Slippy strike up. The Underworld track is his walk on song, unsurprisingly given the Trainspotting connotations. Sunshine on Leith just wouldn’t do it in this context. Fine for after last weekend’s win for Hibs at Hampden against Hearts, but perhaps a little too plaintive, a little too pretty, for a night of good-to-honest testosterone-fuelled violence.

As much as the Easter Road club might wish otherwise, a line – if admittedly indirect - can be drawn between Hibs and Knox’s prowess in the ring.

“Before I got into bare knuckle, I boxed," he says. "But I was also fighting in football as well. I grew up in a place where there was a lot of gang culture, an environment of violence as a young boy. You had to be streetwise from a young age I suppose.”

He ran with the Capital City Service – Hibs casuals – from 17-years-old onwards. “I found it attractive,” he says. “It was something I felt I was missing: being part of a brotherhood, a group.”

The casuals scene, including rival groups such as ASC (Aberdeen Soccer Casuals) and Dundee Utility, seems to belong to another era of Scottish football. But the fights still happen, just further away from stadiums.

“Anywhere there isn’t cameras,” he says. “Back in the 80s and most of the 90s, you had groups of a hundred plus. Since TV took over, groups became smaller, more confined, more secret. You had to move with the times I suppose. You couldn’t wander around in groups of hundreds, you’d be seen by all the cameras.”

It became violence by appointment. “You’d phone and say: ‘Meet you there’. A set meet. Sometimes nothing would ever happen. You’d phone all day and nothing would happen. But you’d still have that day out with your mates.”

The arrangements could be painstaking. “It was all planned out. You needed management skills! A lot went into it. You didn’t just turn up and fight. What time were we fighting, what time were the trains, what pubs to go to? It was a lot of work. I could probably pass a management course now quite easily!”

Unlike some, Knox would always endeavour to go the game. He was and remains a football fan and played at a decent level. His first ambition was to be a professional footballer. He played for Hawick Royal Albert and Coldstream in the East of Scotland league – on the wing, wing-back or full-back and then, latterly, as a sweeper with a reputation for never shirking a tackle.

“I was still involved in the casuals scene, still attracted to it,” he says. “It was not all about violence. It was about being with your mates. We dressed the same, liked the same music. Away days having a laugh. It wasn’t all about fighting.”

But some of it undoubtedly was. He remembers once playing in a Scottish Junior Cup tie for Newcraighall, which had kicked off early. “I then went home and got changed before a fight with Raith Rovers, who were playing a Scottish Cup tie against Hibs,” he says.

“Everyone has got a bit older now. The scene has changed. Six guys v six guys, in a forest somewhere. Last team standing wins. To me that’s not football casuals, because you are away from everything.”

“I am not saying football violence is good,” he says. “But there’s so much more to it. It’s guys looking forward to being with their mates, they maybe need that at the end of the week. It’s a release. It acts as therapy for some people.”

The message is: don’t diss what you’ve never tried. The same could apply to bare-knuckle boxing, which is now edging into the mainstream to the extent that this weekend’s extravaganza has been advertised on the side of buses.

Maybe an invite onto the pitch at Hibs before a game might be forthcoming for Knox – it ought to be if he prevails this weekend, which would mean he is the No. 1 contender for the UK featherweight bare-knuckle title.

His ultimate dream is one likely to give Edinburgh city fathers pause. “My goal, my dream fight, if it wasn't Easter Road, would be on the castle esplanade,” he says. “Outdoors, in the summer. It would have a gladiatorial feel about it, you know? That would just be amazing.”

New museum dedicated to Italian Modernist Mario Sironi must not become ‘endorsement of fascism’, art historians warn

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The museum is expected to be housed in the National Association of War Disabled and Wounded Veterans, a monumental fascist era building inaugurated in 1928

Courtesy of the Italian ministry of culture

Plans by Giorgia Meloni’s government to open a new museum in Rome dedicated to Mario Sironi, the Modernist painter who was a supporter of Benito Mussolini, risk sanitising Italy's fascist past, two leading historians have warned.

The state-run museum is expected to open next year within the Associazione Nazionale fra Mutilati ed Invalidi di Guerra (National Association of War Disabled and Wounded Veterans), a monumental fascist era building inaugurated in 1928. The building is home to two major frescoes by Sironi painted ten years later. One depicts Mussolini on horseback.

The museum will display 30 works recently acquired by Italy's culture ministry from the Mario Sironi Archive in Rome, including paintings, drawings and preparatory sketches. A further 90 works will be loaned by the archive.

Unveiling the plans on Tuesday, Alessandro Giuli, the culture minister, said the project was “the defining achievement of my term in office, and the one of which I am most proud”.

Meloni's Brothers of Italy has political roots in the postwar Italian neo-fascist movement. Press materials for the museum do not mention fascism or Mussolini.

Sironi joined the fascist movement in 1919, contributing more than 1,700 political cartoons and illustrations to Il Popolo d’Italia, Mussolini’s newspaper. The museum will feature works spanning more than four decades from the 1910s to the 1950s, covering distinct phases from Sironi's artistic development including Futurism, metaphysical-influenced works and monumental commissions awarded under the fascist regime.

Courtesy of the Italian ministry of culture

Members of Italy’s cultural sector have emphasised the need for the institution to address Seroni’s politics.

Tomaso Montanari, an art historian and rector of the Università per Stranieri di Siena (University for Foreigners of Siena), said that he did not oppose the museum but insisted it must explicitly distance itself from fascism.

“Since Italy is a republic founded on anti-fascism, the museum's educational material must make clear that there is no endorsement of fascism," Montanari told The Art Newspaper.

Montanari, who sits on the culture ministry's scientific committee, said that the initial project for the museum focussed “overwhelmingly” on Sironi's fascist period work. He added that the ministry "partially" heeded his argument for works from other periods to be included.

Mario Sironi, Autoritratto (Self-portrait)

Courtesy of the Italian ministry of culture

The art historian and curator Fabio Benzi, who is on the Sironi museum's scientific committee, told the French-language publication Le Journal Des Arts: "The danger is of legitimising a certain authoritarian aesthetic under the guise of cultural rediscovery".

The culture ministry has been contacted for comment.

Last year, the ministry announced it had acquired the former Rome home of Giacomo Balla, the futurist painter who initially supported fascism, which operates as a museum.

On Thursday [5 November], key members of Meloni's government presented a new exhibition celebrating the 90th anniversary of the EUR district in Rome, the city's grandest example of fascist-era planning.

The inverted photo of the Italian prime minister has drawn comparisons to images of Benito Mussolini's execution

Alarmed post-colonial researchers have raised concerns over plans to open Museo Italo Africano "Ilaria Alpi" in late 2021

Curators of the controversial Salò institution say museum will “neither demonise nor defend” fascist leader Benito Mussolini

Feds Unveil Plan to Overhaul National Wildlife Refuges, Get More ‘Boots on the Ground’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 6, 2026 1:06 PM EST

Get the hottest outdoor news from Outdoor Life.

Following a 6-month review of the operation, purpose, and function of America’s National Wildlife Refuges, the U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service today announced an initiative to improve habitat, expand refuge access, and redirect resources to field operations.

Results of the internal refuge review were greeted with relief by wildlife organizations, some of which worried that the Trump administration intended to divest some refuges or to weaken their role as America’s top-tier wildlife properties.

In comments this morning at the Audubon NWR in North Dakota, both Secretary of the Interior Doug Burgum and USFWS Director Brian Nesvik outlined 29 recommendations that generally support refuge field operations, improve both public access and administrative efficiency, and boost law enforcement resources.

However, with a 20 percent cut in funding and the loss of nearly a third of its workforce, it’s unclear how the Service will implement some of today’s directives.

The recommendations announced today are compiled in a program called Rooted In Purpose that generally increases the Department of Interior’s investments in the National Wildlife Refuge System that includes 573 refuges across 95 million acres of land. Nesvik announced the initiative in December 2025 and tapped fellow Wyomingite and senior advisor Joshua Coursey to conduct the 6-month review. Nesvik further detailed the background and sideboards of the refuge review in a wide-ranging interview with Outdoor Life in January 2026.

“Our purpose is simply to confirm that [each] refuges’ original purpose still aligns with the Service’s current mission,” Nesvik told Outdoor Life in January. “People have assumed that means we’re looking for ways to sell land. That is not the intent. The intent is to make sure we’re still doing those things that were intended when the refuge was created but also to make sure that things aren’t completely askew from our current mission.”

Today’s announcement, which is also detailed in a Secretarial Order entitled “Making America’s National Wildlife Refuges Great Again” issued by Burgum, distilled the 6-month review into four main areas of focus that the Service will apply over the next two years.

Nesvik noted today that his Director’s Order 230 resulted in 41 in-person refuge visits, 118 virtual visits, and four regional office visits along with internal surveys that solicited more than 1,000 responses from USFWS staff, and an external survey that received more than 2,000 responses.

“Refuge employees know what the land needs and what it takes to get the work done,” Nesvik said today in remarks delivered at the North Dakota refuge. “Their experience shaped this review, and their input will help us put its recommendations into action. We are focused on giving them practical tools and clear guidance to manage habitat, protect wildlife and provide Americans with quality hunting, fishing and other outdoor opportunities.”

Today’s news was generally welcomed by wildlife organizations.

“The USFWS refuge system has been an important part of the conservation successes in North America,” said Corey Mason, CEO and executive vice president of conservation for the Wild Sheep Foundation. “We applaud Secretary Burgum and Director Nesvik’s efforts to refocus the refuge system on the field work necessary to ensure quality habitat for wildlife and opportunity for Americans to benefit from that work and access these unique places.”

Ariel Weigard, vice president for government affairs for Pheasants Forever and Quail Forever commended Burgum and Nesvik “for their efforts to strengthen the National Wildlife Refuge System and its ability to deliver conservation on the ground.”

Today’s announcement didn’t detail how the Rooted in Purpose initiative will be funded and implemented. The USFWS budget has been cut by about 20 percent, or some $323 million in the Trump administration’s 2027 budget. Programs that have been either cut or eliminated next year include state and tribal wildlife grants, migratory bird conservation funds, and the entire National Wildlife Refuge Fund.

The Rooted in Purpose program provides some insights in how funding might be redirected to achieve the goals of refuge reform. The document details the Service intends to eliminate the Urban Wildlife Conservation Program Policy and “redirect all discretionary program funding to activities that directly support the conservation mission.”

The directive also prioritizes what it calls “boots on the ground.”

Read Next: The Rush to Build Data Centers on Public Land Is Worse Than You Thought

“Reduce headquarters and regional office staffing and reallocate positions to refuge operations, prioritizing the people needed to manage habitat, maintain facilities, support public access and protect resources.”

Lastly, the Service intends to stretch limited resources by using “technology and Super Intelligence to improve efficiency” and to promote contracting and reducing barriers for “for qualified local businesses competing for federal work.”

Andrew McKean is Outdoor Life’s hunting and conservation editor, drilling into issues that affect wildlife, wildlands, and the people who care about them. He’s also OL’s optics editor, helping readers to make informed buying decisions.

Solana's Skyline Crypto Ad Triggers Central Bank Investigation in China

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A joint operation of the People's Bank of China with Shanghai police clamped down on the Solana Accelerate China 2026 four-day tour. Authorities blocked the project's events in Shanghai, while the financial regulator in parallel launched an investigation against those locals who helped the blockchain platform with logistics and public relations.

What drew such a harsh reaction from the Chinese authorities was 'too aggressive' offline marketing of the tour. On the evening of October 14, a team associated with Solana, together with Shanghai contractors, put giant vibrant logos of the SOL token on the Citigroup Tower skyscraper facade in Lujiazui - the very heart of Shanghai's financial district. This was not just an ad: top managers, including Solana Foundation president Lily Lu, started publicly boasting about the opening of Shanghai's new office in a prestigious building.

All this massive campaign predictably caused an excitement in crypto circles. Even Binance founder Changpeng Zhao, known simply as CZ, expressed his amusement, calling 'amazing' the fact that Solana managed to not only do a promo in China, but get an office there as well. BNB Chain did not achieve such a success, added CZ.

It seems like the organizers tried to bypass the regulatory barriers using a positioning trick. The four-day tour between Shanghai, Hangzhou, Shenzhen, and Beijing was marketed strictly around technologies like AI agents, robots, IT development - not about crypto at all.

The maneuver did not get recognition in China to say the least, and the backlash did not take long to follow.

The morning session on October 16 in Shangri-La was shut down outright, with participants held for over an hour on the street after police suddenly arrived. Lily Lu tried to narrow the angles and stated that the cancellation was caused by registrations quadrupling, so the site was not ready to invite all registrants.

Fast forward to November 6 and representatives of the People's Bank of China, in an exclusive interview with the state agency Financial News, finally ended speculations on what actually happened. He directly called the skyline ad and all the aligned events an illegal marketing of virtual currencies. The Central Bank's position is 'black and white' there: SOL is a cryptocurrency and Solana's infra not only facilitates coins issuance, but also helps stablecoin turnover (especially USDT), which is banned.

Organizers in the face of Solana Foundation breached the crypto-risk agreement jointly issued back in February 2026 by eight Chinese authority departments. The document directly bans all the promotion of overseas crypto projects and implements individual liability for Chinese citizens and companies that help to do this.

Solana Foundation hasn't provided any official comments on the matter yet.

The signal by People's Bank of China is unambiguous though: full cleanup of all the mainland market out of foreign blockchain networks and activities associated with them.

Premier League player stopped by armed police for carrying bow and arrows in public

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A Premier League soccer player was stopped by police in England after being spotted carrying a bow and arrows near a reservoir, prompting a response reportedly involving six police cars.

Amar Dedic, a 24-year-old defender for Newcastle United, was with a friend in Northumberland, England, when a member of the public spotted them carrying the equipment and alerted authorities.

According to reports, six police vehicles were dispatched to the area, including armed officers, who eventually stopped the pair in a car around seven miles from where they were first seen.

Police searched the vehicle and confiscated the bow and arrows, but determined that neither man posed a threat to the public.

In a statement, Northumbria Police confirmed that officers had responded to reports of two men carrying a bow and arrows while walking near a reservoir in the Harlow Hill area.

“Officers attended and subsequently stopped a vehicle in the Darras Hall area and seized a bow and arrows,” police said.

“At this stage, it is understood they were for recreational use and there was no threat to the public.”

Police said their enquiries were ongoing, although they did not publicly identify Dedic in their statement.

BBC Sport reported that the soccer player had no malicious intentions and fully cooperated with officers throughout the incident.

By chance, two other professional soccer players, Newcastle goalkeeper Mark Gillespie and former defender Paul Dummett, reportedly arrived while police were speaking to Dedic.

Dedic joined Newcastle United in August 2026 for a reported transfer fee of around £30 million ($39 million).

The Bosnia-Herzegovina international has made 10 appearances for the club across league and cup competitions since his arrival. Newcastle United has not publicly commented on the incident.

First Portugal call-up for Monza’s Varela alongside Mora, Ramos and Tavares

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

In-form Monza striker Gustavo Varela has earned his first call-up to the Portugal national team for November’s Nations League fixtures, along with Roma teenager Rodrigo Mora, Milan striker Goncalo Ramos and Lazio full-back Nuno Tavares.

Portugal will be in Nations League action against Denmark on Saturday, November 14 and away against Wales on Tuesday, November 17 and will have four Serie A stars to call upon.

There has been a first call-up for 21-year-old Monza striker Varela, who leads the Serie A goalscoring charts with nine goals from his first nine league appearances of the 2026-27 campaign. With cup fixtures added in, he is now up to 11 goals from his first 10 competitive Monza appearances.

19-year-old Rodrigo Mora has also taken well to life in Italy and has provided three goals from his first eight appearances in Serie A since joining from Porto during the summer transfer window. He could be set to earn his first caps for his nation this break, although this is not his first international call-up.

Goncalo Ramos became Milan’s all-time most expensive signing when he joined from Paris Saint-Germain over the summer and has kept his place in the Portugal national team squad for November. He has scored three goals and provided one assist from his first 13 appearances with the Rossoneri.

There is also room for Nuno Tavares, who has started the last nine Serie A matches in a row. His Lazio side sit at the top of the Serie A table with 28 points from a possible 30 after the first 10 league matches of the season.

Goalkeepers: Diogo Costa (Porto), Rui Silva (Sporting CP), Samuel Soares (Benfica).

Defenders: Diogo Dalot (Manchester United), Alberto Costa (Porto), Ruben Dias (Manchester City), Tomas Araujo (Benfica), Renato Veiga (Villarreal), Antonio Silva (Bournemouth), Joao Cancelo (Barcelona), Nuno Tavares (Lazio).

Midfielders: Joao Palhinha (Benfica), Ruben Neves (Al-Hilal), Mateus Fernandes (Tottenham), Vitinha (PSG), Joao Neves (PSG), Bernardo Silva (Real Madrid).

Forwards: Pedro Neto (Chelsea), Francisco Trincao (Al-Ahli), Carlos Forbs (Club Brugge), Rafael Leao (Galatasaray), Geovany Quenda (Chelsea), Bruno Fernandes (Manchester United), Joao Felix (Al-Nassr), Rodrigo Mora (Roma), Goncalo Ramos (Milan), Fabio Silva (Borussia Dortmund), Gustavo Varela (Monza).

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Sydney Powerhouse scandal, heritage destruction in Lebanon, Dan Flavin—podcast

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Powerhouse Parramatta opens in Sydney on 7 November

From breaking news and insider insights to exhibitions and events around the world, the team at The Art Newspaper picks apart the art world’s big stories with the help of special guests. An award-winning podcast hosted by Ben Luke.

This weekend, Powerhouse Parramatta, a vast new arts and technology museum, opens in Sydney, Australia. But its unveiling is shrouded in allegations of misconduct relating to the chief executive who had led the project, Lisa Havilah, who stepped down in August. Ben Luke speaks to The Art Newspaper’s Australian reporter, Elizabeth Fortescue, about the building and the scandal.

A view from inside the museum, which will be the largest in New South Wales

In southern Lebanon, despite a supposed ceasefire in the war between Hezbollah and Israel, heritage continues to be damaged by Israeli military activity. Ben talks to Sarvy Geranpayeh, who has been talking to politicians, heritage professionals and artists about the destruction.

The 18th-century St George’s Melkite Catholic church in Dardghaya in the Tyre district was severely damaged in Israeli attacks in October 2024

And this episode’s Work of the Week is greens crossing greens (to Piet Mondrian who lacked green) (1966) by Dan Flavin. A landmark work for the US artist, first shown at the Van Abbemuseum in Eindhoven, it is one of the highlights of the exhibition Dan Flavin: Some Light at the Guggenheim Museum in Bilbao, Spain, which opens next week. Ben discusses the work with Lauren Hinkson, curator for collections at the Solomon R. Guggenheim Museum, New York, who is organising the Bilbao show.

How to trade all the Masks from the Happy Mask Shop in Zelda Ocarina of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Skull Mask, the Spooky Mask, the Bunny Hood, and more are part of the Happy Mask Shop quest in OOT Remake

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Happy Mask Shop in Zelda Ocarina of Time has you run around Hyrule trading masks with characters for Rupees, including the Skull Mask, Spooky Mask, Bunny Hood, and the Keaton mask. Though these masks only serve as commodities to be traded with specific characters, if you can allocate all four to the right characters and complete this OOT quest, the Happy Mask Shop will give you access to four new masks that all have a very specific utility. Below I'll show you how to hand out all the Masks and the specific locations you need to look.

Keep in mind that the Switch 2 version is the same as the original, in that the Happy Mask Shop won't open in The Legend of Zelda Ocarina of Time until you open the passage to the Goron City. Fortunately, the guard who opens the passage will actually tell you that the Mask Shop is now open, flagging that you can start this questline.

Bought from the Mask Shop for 10 Rupees, you need to give this to the Guard in Kakariko Village who opens the passage for you, the same one who lets you know that the Mask Shop is open. He'll give you 15 Rupees in return.

Bought for 20 Rupees, head back to the starting area of Kokiri Village, where you start off and get the first Shield and Sword in Zelda Ocarina of Time, and turn North into the Lost Woods.

The moment you enter, head left through the hollow trunk to meet The Skull Kid, playing a tune on top of a log. He, understandably, will offer to take the Skull Mask from you, though only pay you ten for it. Cheapskate.

After buying the Spooky Mask for 30 Rupees, head back to Kakariko Village, and this time take the East exit to the Graveyard, but do so during daytime. Here you'll find a boy wandering around. Speak to him to give him the Spooky Mask, for which he'll give you 30 Rupees back.

The final mask in this sequence is bought for 50 Rupees. You can only sell it once you've progressed to the point where you have obtained the Three Spiritual Stones in the main story.

Sign up to our Switch 2 Spotlight newsletter to make sure you get the very latest news on Nintendo's new console and its games every week

Once this happens, head to Hyrule Field. There'll be a man who runs in circles around the field, practicing his running. If you go during the night, he'll be sitting beside the road and you won't have to keep pace. Either way, if you give him the Bunny Hood, he will entirely fill your wallet with Rupees, so it's a good idea to have expanded it as much as possible prior to the trade to really capitalize on that.

Having given these masks out, the Happy Mask Shop will now have four new masks in store that you can get, all of which serve a particular function. Here's what they do:

It's really only the Mask of Truth that provides the major advantage to gameplay, though some of the dialogue options and reactions from NPCs can prompt interesting lore information. Likewise, the cash infusion delivered by the Bunny Hood NPC can make Link incredibly rich, a great reward in and of itself.

Google's Find Hub can finally notify you if you've left a tracker behind

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The company has finally introduced a ‘Notify when left behind’ notification for tracking devices

Google has started rolling out a feature for the Find Hub that will send you a notification if you've left a location tracker — or the item you've attached it to — behind. Apple has had separation alerts for years, letting you enable them for Find My-enabled tracking devices so you can quickly get notified when they're not with you. Samsung added the feature to SmartThings for devices like its Galaxy SmartTags and Buds a few years ago. With separation alerts enabled, you may have better chances of retrieving an item you've dropped or forgotten, say, before you get too far or before you've boarded your plane.

The feature's rollout was first reported by 9to5Google. To get separation alerts on your Android phone, open the Find Hub app, choose a tag from your device list, then select the Settings gear icon. On the tracker's page, you'll see a new section where you can enable Notify when left behind for that specific device. If you switch it on, you'll get an alert from the Find Hub network if you get far away enough from the tracker, unless you've left it at your designated Home address on Google Maps.

Currently, the feature seems to be live in the US and in some parts of Europe. It also seems available across brands, with 9to5Google able to access the feature with their Moto and Chipolo trackers linked to an Android phone.

Oppo confirms its next foldable will actually know what a ‘global’ release is

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Oppo Find N6 is a stunning foldable but, for the second year in a row, it really stretches the definition of what a “global” release means. Oppo Find N7, apparently, will fix that.

Oppo Find N5 and Find N6 both saw global launches, but the company’s definition of that excluded Europe and virtually every other major market. For the most part, both foldables were essentially sold only in China, with major markets elsewhere left out. And that’s a shame, because both raised the bar for foldables in major ways.

Now, we’re looking forward to the launch of Oppo Find N7, a device rumored to be adopting the wide form factor popularized by Galaxy Z Fold 8 and iPhone Duo, and that Oppo technically offered before anyone else.

And it might actually get a global release.

Speaking to Nixanbal (via GSMArena), Oppo apparently confirmed that its next foldable will see a release in Europe. It’d be the first time an Oppo foldable made its way to the region, with the OnePlus Open serving as Oppo’s global foldable back in 2023 for its one and only generation.

As for a release date, that’s still unconfirmed, but Oppo tends to release new foldables early in the year, so March 2027, give or take, is probably when we’ll see more officially.
